# Task0034 — P0 expiring heater authorization and bounded power-off

Status: IN_PROGRESS; manager/worker explicitly authorized to start 2026-11-25. Highest priority before Task0032 and Task0033 production integration. Owner reports repeated sustained heater power during historical crashes; a failed print is acceptable, uncontrolled heating is not. No live hardware test/flash until separate owner approval. Safety before ISR optimization.

## Scope and gates

Own heater power output in HAL, thermal authorization in TemperatureControl, watchdog/timebase/periodic service as necessary, and task-specific tests. Coordinate common files with Task0032 (#101 research held for production) and Task0033 (research only); #91 remains held. No unrelated motion, UART, PID tuning or fan behavior changes. Preserve four boards, EEPROM/protocol, calibrated PID/autotune, manual heat, pause, idle heat, scan, and fail-off. Read AGENTS, TASKS, docs/heater-safety.md, Task0010/0021/0027/0029/0030. Use GitHub Actions all host replays and four AVR builds; linked disassembly/latency and independent fault injection. No claim of physical safety until approved hardware gate.

## Confirmed starting point

At f5b86f20f2636228365509448bb9366cf0f69ad4 (docs only since Task0030): HAL.cpp Timer0 COMPB increments PWM counter every nominal 256us, heater frame 32.768ms (128 ticks), latches requestedDuty at start; duty max 254 holds HIGH across frames. A zero demand is not itself an immediate pin write in HAL::setHeaterDuty. Interrupt blackout can stretch HIGH; foreground stall may repeat stale duty indefinitely until watchdog/reset or other protection. Commands::checkForPeriodicalActions renews generic heartbeat on scan tick before manageTemperatures. WDT is interrupt-only, nominal 16ms; pings external WDI using millis age < WATCHDOG_MAIN_LOOP_TIMEOUT (20s), and posts compensation tick. A generic heartbeat is not proof of thermal completion; timebase may lose overflows with IRQ masked. TemperatureControl.cpp normal PID publishes duty per controller, autotune owns demand and can hold it across many loops; explicit renewal after valid completed thermal checks is required for both. Fault latching, zero target, sensor invalid/stale, autotune exit, boot, reset and all other heater pin writers must be inventoried. Do not assert historical crash cause.

## Requirements and architecture options

1. Separate requested duty, fresh valid thermal authorization, physical pulse state and actual delivered energy. New pulses require fresh completed sensor and fault checks; no generic scan heartbeat or repeated ISR rearm of stale authorization. Expiry absolute and wrap-safe with bounded clock progress. Fail closed on missing, stale, invalid or inconsistent state; zero/inhibit must cancel pending/active pulses immediately where CPU running. Fault/timeout must not automatically resume heating on returning foreground after a prolonged stall; latch/recovery policy explicit. All channels independent where useful, global fault stops all. No pulse restart without fresh permission. Preserve normal 100ms controller rate; derive lease and maximum pulse from actual worst healthy service, thermal power/risk and timer resolution, not arbitrary constants. Model full duty as bounded authorization, not perpetual HIGH.
2. Prefer interrupt-independent hardware falling edge (non-PWM compare clear, explicit rearm after checks; verify OC latch setup/COM switching, counter wrap, register update races, actual pin polarity, reset and timer mode). Hotend0 PB4 OC2A, hotend1 PH6 OC2B, RF1000 bed PH5 OC4C; RF2000/v2 bed PE2 has NO OC. Timer2 shared counter, compare priority above Timer1, 8bit /1024 max 16.384ms span; normal mode and independent compare channels need bounded pulse scheduling. RF1000 Timer4 routing differs from RF2000 RGB. No unsafe timer remap or board mods. Hardware auto-clear only while clock and pin peripheral function operate. Generic free-running PWM repeats stale duty and is not a fail-safe. For non-OC bed investigate short dedicated off ISR plus independent hardware reset/gate; no software IRQ alone guarantees off with I=0 or CPU stall. Investigate AVR WDT reset mode/interrupt+reset and external TPS3820-50 wiring/revision/power-stage default-off (published RF2000 WDI PC0 vs firmware PA7 discrepancy). Never continuously feed WDT from mere ISR or generic loop; proof thermal progress, and actual reset drives power stage off including bootloader. Independent physical overtemperature/power cut needed for stuck switch, clock failure, etc. No claim universal guarantee from firmware.
3. Separate heater edge scheduling from ADC, fan, LCD and ticks; compare event-driven frame start + falling edges versus current software counting, preserve average duty/period initially. Timer2 priority useful when IRQs enabled, not preemption or stall immunity. Compare unchanged architecture with explicit lease first, then bounded hardware pulse candidates; no requirement to remove Advance or other IRQs. Consider staggered pulses and safe quantization only after checking power-stage topology, thermal dynamics and PID/autotune delivered duty. Owner notes energy averaging: 10/25/40% over equal periods equals 25% average. Do NOT compensate lost heating across fault or expiry, or allow unbounded energy debt/catch-up. Optional bounded normal-mode quantization correction only with fresh authorization; preserve max power and temperature safety.
4. Reproducible fault model before production changes: normal duty 0..255, full demand, foreground dead while IRQs run, IRQ disabled while HIGH, overload with delayed OFF/ON, timer flag coalescence, stale ADC, valid heartbeat but no completed PID, sensor disconnect/short, stop/inhibit, autotune stuck and exit, reset/brownout/boot, timer wrap, multiple heater channels, renewed/revoked permission race. Distinguish hardware OC clear, ISR-only clear, reset gate assumption. Record actual heater HIGH duration, cumulative energy and expiry/last fresh check to physical-off latency; unsupported physical cases not passes. No energy compensation after failure. Verify normal profiles and timing/CPU cost with equal load and 500k UART stress, no regression to motion pulses or critical service deadlines.

## Staging

A. Trace all current power writers/readers, normal/autotune authorization, watchdog/reset and board routes; record failure paths and executable abstract fault model with counterexamples. B. Prototype per-channel authorization and output architecture privately, validate real source replays and AVR linked cycles; prove timer mode/rearm/expiry, wrap and race behavior. C. Integrate only validated candidates, compare exact latest target with four builds and host suite, record limitations and unresolved physical gate. Separate hardware acceptance later: disconnected heaters/instrumented load first, actual off latency and reset power-stage proof on each model, fault injection and independent overtemperature limit. No firmware deployment now.

### A0 abstract fault model (first executable research)

Added tools/task0034/heater-fault-model.cjs at cfd0e1ca56e0ff5b26deeef0bf0666394df61a7d. Reproduce with node tools/task0034/heater-fault-model.cjs. Function executed in JS tool runtime before commit: 120 cases (4 architectures × 6 fault scenarios × 5 duties), eight explicit assertions passed. This is an abstract counterexample model, not an execution of the AVR firmware, no actual timer flags/clock drift/multiple channels or hardware pin dynamics. Time grid 256us; nominal simulation horizon 400ms rounded up to 400.128ms; fault at 50ms sampled at next tick. Illustrative lease 120ms and hypothetical independent reset at 140ms from last grant are NOT chosen production parameters. Full-duty cases last HIGH end from initial grant: legacy 400.128ms (still high at horizon) for foreground stall, IRQ masked, thermal stall with generic heartbeat alive; ISR lease 120.064ms for foreground/thermal stall but 400.128ms for IRQ masked; modeled hardware compare 120.064ms for foreground/thermal stall, 65.536ms for IRQ masked (last in-flight pulse ends), independent reset model 140.032ms for IRQ masked (assumed reset gate, not board proof). The ordinary ISR-only lease is explicitly rejected as sole fail-off mechanism. Zero duty and explicit revoke assertions passed for modeled candidates. No physical gate assumed verified. Next A1: exact callsite inventory and source-level tests, design hardware compare clear with independent WDT reset for non-OC outputs, model real timer granularity and rollover, healthy duty/autotune behavior, stale sensor generation and stop races. Keep task in draft; do not flash or claim hardware safety.

### A2 independent expiry flag and hardware one-shot candidates

Read actual HAL ADC publication/freshness: 32 conversions/channel, nominal complete scan <=32.768 ms, valid publication <=100 ms via millis, repeated read is non-consuming. This clock depends on Timer0 OVF IRQ; a blackout can coalesce overflow flags. Do not use it as sole independent power-off deadline. Normal control calls setHeaterDuty after per-controller validation; fault and invalid paths zero duty. Autotune sets positive duty at entry and phase changes, not every valid sample; renewal must occur explicitly after its full 100 ms sample/fault/rise checks, not in manageTemperatures's selected autotune skip or generic heartbeat. Its admission reads temperature before UI/serial messages; revalidate before first pulse. Boot first stops external WDT and clears RAM requests, checks board identity before initializing heater pins; hotend pins set output/low later, bed output/low before analogStart; Timer0 setup and watchdog only near end of setup. Reset/bootloader physical fail-off remains separate evidence. Global inhibit flag and stopAllHeaters currently separate operations; assess atomic global output revoke before lease integration.

Candidate free-running Timer3 or Timer5 /256 (16us ticks, 1.048576s wrap), one OCR compare per heater, COM pins disconnected; no periodic expiry IRQ in healthy operation. Sticky OCF preserves elapsed deadline even with I=0 and across multiple wraps; expiry ISR and PWM frame admission check flag and remaining distance. OCF posts on next timer tick after equality; active renewal must not write-one-to-clear a pending expiry. Guard near old deadline, bound snapshot-to-commit (linked cycles) and recheck flag after writing OCR. Clear stale flags only when inactive/off; model initial arm separately. Use latched failure, no automatic renewal. Compare flags are not physical cutoff, lower-priority ISR may be delayed, WDT reset/gate or hardware OC necessary. Exact final model at aae99f25e039b30ef7bd141c5cebe9c00928f226, executed fetched source in JS runtime: 22,937,600 renewal cases, 35 bounded inter-operation tick patterns, 9,175,040 accepted, 13,762,560 rejected, 1,750,000 wrapped extensions; 12 delayed expiry scenarios; counterexamples for equality before flag and naive clearing of active OCF. Model assumes <=3 timer ticks total gap, 12,500 ticks (200ms) illustrative NOT selected lease or measured WCET. Need real initial grant, revoke/multiple channels, write high/low order and AVR flag timing test before production. Script node tools/task0034/lease-timer-model.cjs. Source: https://onlinedocs.microchip.com/oxy/GUID-EC8D3BAB-0B5E-454F-AB6E-6A7C91C6F103-en-US-3/GUID-37947E0D-CC4C-47CD-86D2-6A531520ED55.html and ATmega2560 datasheet normal-mode compare/FOC.

Hardware compare one-shot sequencing must itself survive a crash between register writes. Naive FOC with COM=set and DDR=output can leave HIGH forever if CPU stalls before switching COM=clear. Candidate: while confirmed OFF, PORT latch LOW/no pull-up, DDR input with externally verified OFF bias, set OC latch via FOC, program bounded OCR and clear-mode before enabling output DDR; reject near/missed compare. Clear-on-match repeats only LOW, never autonomous HIGH. Model tools/task0034/heater-one-shot-model.cjs at ec2523b2f2c58b9d6c56a01c7674980a015d6886 executed function before commit: 70,632 crash-prefix/phase/gap cases, 2 naive unsafe counterexamples. 8-bit all 256 start phases for selected widths, 16-bit representative starts; model assumes safe input bias, independent functioning timer, serialization, no prior pulse or arbitrary register corruption. No direct silicon proof or hardware experiment; validate compare latch/DDR semantics and guard with actual emulator/linked source, shared COM/DDR write isolation, no compare reassignment or pin changes yet. Timer2 /1024 max 16.384 ms wrap means existing 32.768 ms heater frame cannot simply be copied as one pulse; split/shorten only with power/duty tests, especially full duty. RF1000 bed Timer4 candidate only if wiring/reset bias confirmed.

Board-specific electrical audit: visually rechecked Conrad RF1000 I851 CTC V2.1 sheet 2 (https://asset.conrad.com/media10/add/160267/c1/-/en/001007508CD01/circuit-diagramm-1007508-renkforce-rf1000-3d-printer.pdf) HZ1/HZ2 MOSFET gates via 51 ohm and LED+680 ohm path; no explicit 100k gate pulldown drawn there. Do NOT assume high-Z safe for those hotends; actual PCB/driver leakage and gate discharge need proof. HZ3 AC bed S202S02F zero-cross SSR, optional HZ3 DC MOSFET and optional PB6/R91 path; actual population/retrofit matters. RF2000 I851 CTC V3.0 sheet 6 (https://asset.conrad.com/media10/add/160267/c1/-/en/001395717CD01/schema-electrique-1395717-renkforce-rf2000-imprimante-3d-appareil-pret-a-lemploi-banc-dimpression-chauffant.pdf) explicitly shows 100k pulldowns R121/122/123 on HZ1/2/3 and HZ3 AC path MOC3061SVM + BTA12-600BWRG, plus optional DC output. Schematic supports reset/input bias hypothesis for that board only, not measured or RF2000v2 proof. RF2000/v2 PE2 lacks OC; WDT reset path remains critical. Sharp S202S02F and MOC3061 zero-cross references https://datasheet.octopart.com/S202S02F-Sharp-Microelectronics-datasheet-8216379.pdf and https://www.onsemi.com/download/application-notes/pdf/an-3006.pdf. For AC bed separate logic pin off from real power off (healthy triac commutates at next current zero, nominal up to 10ms on 50Hz resistive load, no guarantee for failed switch). Actual delivered energy quantized by mains half-waves and phase, not proportional to microsecond GPIO duty in each short frame. Model 50/60Hz arbitrary phase and normal-mode bounded duty averaging; do not assume same strategy as DC hotend, no mains switching changes without topology/EMI/safety review. No physical board, bootloader or mains tests.

Next: task-private actual C++ authorization state and timer adapter, including initial arming/revoke/expiry races, normal/autotune explicit renewals, measure healthy service bound and linked critical sections, qualify AVR simulator; hardware compare output and independent watchdog/reset path separately. Do not merge model-only confidence into claims of physical safety.

### A4 autotune authorization preparation (new candidate)

22db4698748f9d47cdc5cabe5e053a08ae77df44: after admission diagnostics, recheck fresh sample, cutoff, inhibit, sensor/power config and defect flags before first positive duty. Existing autotune relay logic and gains unchanged; publish positive demand at end of each completed 100ms sampled iteration only after cancellation, fault, timing and rise checks, rather than only at phase transitions. Keep draft.

Watchdog: ATmega2560 datasheet WDE enables independent reset, WDRF forces WDE after watchdog reset; interrupt+reset automatically clears WDIE when vector executes, do not blindly re-enable as current interrupt-only ISR does. Arduino stock STK500v2 source documents watchdog-reset handling, historical issue https://github.com/arduino/ArduinoCore-avr/issues/183 demonstrates source/binary version uncertainty. Actual installed RF bootloader/fuses and gate bias not verified by pinned CI core version; firmware-only WDT reset must be treated as unqualified until physical gate. Do not move 16ms compensation period or external WDI without measuring preserved semantics. Candidate reset-only watchdog with foreground progress/thermal proof, separate timer for compensation, no generic ISR feeding; independent compare-clear for OC outputs remains research. See https://developerhelp.microchip.com/xwiki/bin/view/products/mcu-mpu/8-bit-avr/structure/wdt/ and https://www.microchip.com/bin/mchp/product-ds.ATmega2560.pdf .

Added tools/task0034/event-pwm-model.cjs, 243658136b9dd5eeb962807cbce81063150ebd2c, executed its function in JS runtime. Candidate Timer5 16-bit normal /8, tick 0.5us, wrap 32.768ms identical to stock heater frame. Timer3 lease independent. Do not count 97% reduction in total current COMPB calls: ADC/coolers/part fan/UI/ticks remain. 16,128 abstract duty/start-delay/off-delay combinations checked. Preserve exact stock non-full quantized widths (duty & 254)*256 timer ticks; full 254/255 candidate width 65,472 ticks (32us off gap, 0.09765625 percentage-point loss, exploratory 64-tick guard not selected). No late ON when insufficient time before deadline; no catch-up energy. Delayed OFF ISR still stretches pulse; model deliberately does not claim safety under I=0. Pending compare flags must not be erased, missed wrap and active pin must block stale rearm, compare vectors outrank own overflow only on dispatch. Need actual register/flag lifecycle replay and linked timing before implementation. Timer5 is a candidate, not assigned production timer.

These are NOT physical zero-cross switching, thermal or EMI evidence; no modeled commutation window, actual ISR jitter, line voltage or phase tracking. No automatic energy correction or hardware experiment.

Task-private EventHeaterPwm.h (f26a3f5), AvrTimer5Pwm.h (fb9fa5e) implement normal /8 Timer5 overflow frame, absolute per-channel compare deadline, separate mask and sticky flag, OC disconnected. Arm deadline while OFF, clear only inactive stale flag, recheck time/flag/overflow, enable compare before HIGH. Late start skips pulse, no energy catch-up; active pulse at new overflow or compare serviced after wrap causes global latch/off; neither a late revoke nor stale flag reset may conceal a missed OFF. Expiry on Timer3 must shut down both owners (combined probe dispatches pwm.inhibit()). No ISR-only architecture can physically switch off during permanent global interrupt mask. An OFF delayed within same frame can still stretch delivered duty; late service statistics and physical protection required.

tests/heater-event-pwm-prototype.py (d45126d, f752b6a, 570801c) covers 256 duties x 11 frame phases x 2/3 channels (expected 14,080 ordinary cases), stale flags, compare-first/overflow-first dispatch after missed OFF, running-timer delays during OCR/W1C arming, explicit revoke after wrap, and combined actual HeaterLease state (independent clock mock, pending expiry blocks next frame, expiry handler inhibits both owners). tests/heater-event-pwm-avr-probe.py (6498fa7) links both adapters with pinned compiler and retains actual disassembly of Timer3/5 vectors, dummy GPIO only. No printer execution. Earlier run 45737864250 in progress at observation, latest test changes require new CI. Separate arm Guard from FullOffGap (d5b9151): 64 ticks/32us is only a sample parameter, historical isolated motion mask already 594 cycles (~37.125us); full-duty off-service gap must be set independently from measured worst combined latency, no guessed production threshold. Wider gap 256 ticks (128us) prototype check, non-full legacy widths unchanged. Need real combined ISR bounds from next artifact and actual Timer5 compare semantics under emulator before integrating.

Fresh sample contract for next integration: a coherent raw batch plus per-channel publication identity must be captured at conversion, not read a newer unvalidated ADC batch at authorization time. Non-consuming diagnostic/temperature reads must remain possible. A completed normal PID/autotune decision may authorize at most once per validated publication; reject consumed identity, changed sensor/power configuration, expired sample or global fault. Prefer atomic compare-and-consume of exact publication; a newer publication arriving between conversion and authorization may cause a conservative skip, measure healthy service impact. Evaluate generation rollover and independent timebase age, not just current millis (can lose overflows); inactive stale samples after long blackout require independent invalidation/reset. No production lease timeout or Timer5 mapping selected yet. Continue with actual source integration after combined probe, guard and freshness validation. Hardware compare-clear and WDT reset/boot/board safety remain unqualified. PR #102 stays draft, no flash or merge.

### A8 combined event prototype checkpoint, ISR compiler finding and emergency restart

[CI 45737990054](https://github.com/Nibbels/Repetier-Firmware/actions/runs/45737990054) green for head 3cb01164cfbdca2b3685f2f501f3a7fcba1380fa, tested merge b21e93f9545f7ebe4648ed21bc463053b898d4b7. [Artifact 15079592835](https://github.com/Nibbels/Repetier-Firmware/actions/runs/45737990054/artifacts/15079592835), downloaded and independently SHA256 verified c980ff18d5146481802e44ac73c30415b87d874d27a7dcbccd6cc3dc4cc20105; summary failures empty; event prototype 5632 two-channel + 8448 three-channel = 14080 ordinary width/phase cases plus focused delay, wrap and combined lease tests passed. Standalone combined AVR probe linked; no production timer changes. Green CI is not a sufficient acceptance gate: manual review of its actual disassembly (SHA256 6cf1a0a9c58f2638253610b8beb1a7bf55528e27f5711c32f572e22cecd78d46) found the compiler emits CALL __vector_32 at 0x744 and 0x794 in __vector_34 and __vector_33. Called vector ends in RETI (0x71c), which can enable interrupts before outer epilogue, introducing unintended nesting/stack risk. This occurs on isolated research binary with identical expiry handlers, not a demonstrated production fault. Local direct-address audit of all four production disassemblies in same artifact: seven linked ISR entry addresses each, zero direct CALL/RCALL to ISR entries. Retained audit script tools/task0034/audit-isr-calls.py (3dd454c), --include-probe exposes old probe failure. Audit is limited to direct call targets; does not prove indirect targets or overall nesting correctness.

Fix 474a423fe5463f9bb76940526ba00dd2a2ab64c9: explicit AVR-libc ISR_ALIASOF for Timer3 B/C to A; combined probe checks equal nm addresses and rejects any direct CALL/RCALL to a vector. New linked evidence required; do not reuse old cycle bounds for changed probe. This avoids calling a RETI handler as an ordinary function; distinct vectors remain separate where channel-specific. Test actual linked machine code for every ISR change in production.

Emergency stop audit: Commands::emergencyStop calls HAL::resetHardware, legacy resetFunc() jump to address zero, not a true peripheral reset. Old implementation did not explicitly revoke heater pins before jumping. Candidate a4941e2b11585ce80ad58986f20d89566f496ff2: globally mask IRQs, call zero-duty revoke for every heater, disable Timer0 COMPB (preserve other TIMSK0 bits), then existing restart transfer; fail closed if it returns. This is an explicit software emergency path, not watchdog/clock-failure protection; does not assert reset of other peripherals, actual bootloader or power-stage behavior. Host extraction in tests/heater-revoke.py (93424821f) verifies all outputs and requests low and PWM compare mask clear at transfer, initially I=0 and I=1, one/two extruders; checks new frame cannot revive old demand. Pending CI. No actual printer restart.

### A9 exact ADC publication reservation (research)

tools/task0034/HeatSampleGate.h (4c3a0ef) implements one noncopyable/nonmovable scoped foreground reservation per channel, 3 state bits (fresh/busy/matching) in one byte, raw and timestamp each uint16_t. A new published batch invalidates the outstanding reservation even if the value is identical; it does not release ownership. Consume succeeds once only for the exact current still-fresh batch, release preserves newer unconsumed publication. No generation-number ABA after arbitrary numbers of publications. Independent 16-bit counter + sticky overflow flag, exclusive flag owner, invalidate all batch reservations whenever wrap observed; deliberately conservative skip around wrap, avoid relying on IRQ-dependent millis. 6250 ticks at /256 = 100ms age is illustrative; actual oldest conversion within batch, service bound, frame/lease commit races and timer ownership must still be qualified. Counter running assumption, no guarantee with permanent CPU stall, arbitrary register corruption or clock failure. All operations including reserve/release and consume+grant require serialized short IRQ-protected sections, conversion outside lock. Capture actual raw data in coherent snapshot, do not use unvalidated newer raw. Must additionally bind sensor type/power limit and completed fault checks at real normal PID and autotune callsites. Prototype owns raw and timestamps; when integrating replace rather than duplicate existing ADC state, quantify SRAM and ISR cost. No production ADC API changes yet.

tests/heater-sample-gate-prototype.py (2ca449b) expected 65536 start counts x seven ages x 2+3+4 channels = 4,128,768 age/phase cases, plus equal-value publications, nested reservation rejection, up to 131073 supersessions, multiple wraps, independent channels and invalidation. Expected count, not executed result yet. Latest observed run 45739785681 (474a423f) in progress, 45739649069 superseded; subsequent audit/doc head needs latest CI. No questions requiring owner decision; no merge/flash. Next verify all new CI, audit alias disassembly and linked bounds, then integrate a narrow source-level grant/expiry candidate with coherent sample ownership and explicit fault handling (simulation only), while pursuing independent physical-off strategy. Do not keep expanding unrelated prototypes; prioritize usable integrated safety path and measure production service timing before selecting timeout.

### A15 bootloader compatibility blocker — owner requested historical research (2026-11-26)

Actual upstream Intel HEX blobs fetched from GitHub, record checksums verified, decoded type-02 segment addresses (0x30000 base). All cover boot section starting byte 0x3E000. Arduino IDE [1.0.5](https://github.com/arduino/Arduino/blob/1.0.5/hardware/arduino/bootloaders/stk500v2/stk500boot_v2_mega2560.hex), 1.0.6 (same path) and pinned [ArduinoCore-avr 1.8.6](https://github.com/arduino/ArduinoCore-avr/blob/1.8.6/bootloaders/stk500v2/stk500boot_v2_mega2560.hex) contain byte-identical HEX blob 68d2e18b3b086643f516677313a2b8f5dff13611, 7454 programmed bytes through 0x3FD1D. [2011-06-29 production binary](https://github.com/arduino/ArduinoCore-avr/blob/1.8.6/bootloaders/stk500v2/Mega2560-prod-firmware-2011-06-29.hex) blob 4f366997c8dfc9bb00bf998fcdfd1e0bff5941d5, 8154 bytes through 0x3FFD9, no aligned WDR opcode 0x95A8 found (not full control-flow proof). [goodHexFiles variant](https://github.com/arduino/Arduino-stk500v2-bootloader/blob/master/goodHexFiles/stk500boot_v2_mega2560.hex) blob acd0c6bf278878d4b68c397594ef6f4f6fedb796, 7434 bytes through 0x3FD09. Contains WDR at 0x3E70A; same EICALL sequence below shifted 8 bytes. No proof of actual Conrad factory binaries or version by model/date. Conrad application update instructions and RF1000 wiki establish Arduino bootloader/serial upload, not factory bootloader image; RF2000v2 forum user reported ISP bootloader replacement in 2020 (https://rf1000.de/viewtopic.php?t=1472&start=30), not a reliable original version or proof of WDT cause.

Critical static opcode finding in 68d2e18b blob: reset vector JMP to 0x3E312; startup 0x3E31E e001 LDI r16,1; 0x3E320 bf0c OUT 0x3c,r16 (EIND=1); startup BSS clearing 0x3E33C..0x3E34A clears SRAM 0x0200..0x020D; main startup 0x3E6FE b794 IN r25,MCUSR; 0x3E700 CLI, 0x3E702 WDR, 0x3E704 clears MCUSR, WDTCSR timed writes 0x3E70C..0x3E712, 0x3E714 SEI; 0x3E716 ff93 SBRS r25,3 (WDRF); 0x3E718 C005 skips branch if not WDRF; 0x3E71A 91e0 0200 LDS ZL,0x200, 0x3E71E 91f0 0201 LDS ZH,0x201; 0x3E722 9519 EICALL. Source declares app_start pointer=0 and calls it on WDRF. EICALL uses EIND:Z as word PC, therefore EIND=1 and Z=0 gives word 0x10000 / byte 0x20000, not app reset vector. This static path can enter arbitrary code inside our >128 KiB application AFTER disabling WDT. Not just reset-loop availability issue. Source/issue https://github.com/arduino/ArduinoCore-avr/issues/183 (2013-12-23), instruction semantics https://onlinedocs.microchip.com/oxy/GUID-0B644D8F-67E7-49E6-82C9-1B2B9ABE6A0D-en-US-23/GUID-3AB5E4EE-020C-4AB1-B9D6-CCB05A106C04.html and https://gcc.gnu.org/onlinedocs/gcc/AVR-Options.html. This is decoded actual opcode evidence, not end-to-end emulation. No claim every modern Mega has this binary.

Known alternate [Nick Gammon corrected binary](https://github.com/nickgammon/arduino_sketches/blob/master/Atmega_Board_Programmer/bootloader_atmega2560_v2.h) header dated 2013-12-15, blob 3ac0061f91c091ce1996be0253de8ffb2e18dbff, 7474 explicit bytes (header labels 8192-byte section MD5 12AA80074D74E3DABF2D25846D99F720, not independently recomputed). Actual array opcodes 0x3E702 WDR, 0x3E704 clears MCUSR, two WDTCSR unlock/zero sequences 0x3E70C..0x3E720, WDRF branch 0x3E726, RWW enable then 0x3E732 CLR ZL, 0x3E734 CLR ZH, 0x3E736 9409 IJMP (ignores EIND), correct application byte 0. Still clears MCUSR before app; our .init3 WDRF latch cannot rely on reading it after such bootloader. Candidate only; no flash, no all-path qualification.

Design consequence: keep reset-WDT experiment disabled on unidentified bootloader; do not use a deliberate watchdog reset as a detection test. User explicitly concerned about different manufacturing dates/replacement boards. Future read-only inventory: determine BOOTSZ/BOOTRST/lock bits, full boot section hash via ELPM if BLB12 permits, or read-only serial bootloader / ISP where supported. Default Arduino lock byte 0x0F prevents application ELPM boot read; do not assume firmware can identify every bootloader. Unknown or denied read remains unknown, never erase/unlock for inspection. Actual factory images not found in public sources searched. Historical external-WDT workaround was discussed May 2011 (https://forum.arduino.cc/t/exteral-watchdog-timer-ic-for-mega2560/58129), plausible Conrad rationale, not evidence of design intent. Favor independently qualifying TPS3820 WDI->RESET path with internal WDE disabled, driven static WDI on stalled progress (not high impedance, which disables supervisor watchdog), so external reset need not take WDRF-specific wrong jump. Confirm RF2000 drawing/firmware routing mismatch and v2. External reset still needs electrical default-off validation, reset source semantics, known bootloader pin behavior, no automatic reheat, independent overtemperature protection. Do not promise physical shutdown solely on reset. No production code changes in this research checkpoint. 

### A16 application-side 0x20000 recovery trampoline proposal (owner question)

Historical match much stronger than generic watchdog reports: original [Arduino forum thread 2013-12-11](https://forum.arduino.cc/t/watchdog-with-the-mega-does-not-work/198985) reports the "goodHexFiles" bootloader works for a small sketch but hangs after watchdog on a 175 kB application. [Gammon's 2013-12-14 correction](https://forum.arduino.cc/t/watchdog-with-the-mega-does-not-work/198985/26) removes app_start(), replaces with CLR Z/IJMP, also adds wdt_disable() and boot_rww_enable(); large reproduction subsequently restarts. [2016-02-10..13 report](https://forum.arduino.cc/t/arduino-mega-watchdog-and-bootloader/364575) sporadic hangs on larger sketches (~160 kB), on Mega R3/Genuino/clone, normal power-cycle works, watchdog interrupt works; replaced WDRF app_start exit with normal IJMP/RWW exit, problem disappeared. Reporter suspected RWW; changes not isolated. Size dependence strongly consistent with A15 wrong-target opcode path, but cannot attribute every historical failure or isolate RWW contribution. Very old WDT reset-loop bootloader (no WDR opcode) is a distinct issue and cannot be rescued by an app trampoline if bootloader never transfers control.

Owner idea: reserve application BYTE 0x20000 (word PC 0x10000), put absolute JMP 0 there, no bootloader replacement. In investigated 68d2e18b binary EICALL uses EIND=1 and Z=0; direct JMP ignores EIND. Prefer six-byte minimal research stub CLI; JMP 0 (f8 94 0c 94 00 00), no RET, no stack use; never overwrite linked code after build. For real safety use fail-closed entry and durable reset reason/marker (bootloader clears MCUSR) before restart, confirm marker survives CRT/boot and is consumed before any heater admission; potentially trap with outputs held safely off instead of automatic restart. Need qualify RWW availability, watchdog state, register/pin conditions, normal boot and reset paths. No guarantee for a bootloader that never reaches app or app Flash unavailable, unknown variants, power stage bias, arbitrary corruption. Correct bootloader enters byte 0 and bypasses stub. Do not enable internal WDE by default on unknown bootloader based solely on this workaround.

Inspected actual standard four ELF disassemblies and linker maps in verified A12 artifact 15083670426 (SHA256 recorded A13). Byte 0x20000 is occupied on all: RF1000 Printer::showConfiguration() at 0x1FE04..0x20134, LDI r25,0x9D; RF2000 PrintLine::prepareQueueMove at 0x1ECE4..0x202D8, MOV r25,r6; v2 same function 0x1ECE8..0x202DC, MOVW r14,r12; X44 GCode::printCommand at 0x1FDA4..0x2001A, LDD r25,Y+1. Actual CRT .init2 in all four: clears r1/SREG, initializes SP to 0x21FF and writes EIND=0 before .data copy/BSS initialization. Thus no need to return through bootloader EICALL stack, normal startup repairs stack/EIND if reached. Not a peripheral reset merely from JMP; preceding WDT reset provides that hardware event.

Link feasibility based on actual map (pinned avr-gcc 7.3.0-atmel3.6.1-arduino7, LTO partitions emit large indivisible .text input sections). End first ltrans0 .text / padding to byte 0x20000: RF1000 0x1CF62 / 12446 B; RF2000 0x1E4C4 / 6972 B; v2 0x1E4C8 / 6968 B; X44 0x1D9D6 / 9770 B. A minimal custom linker-script split after first LTO partition, fill gap, KEEP named recovery section at exact address, remaining .text afterwards, suggests 12452/6978/6974/9776 B extra Flash including 6B stub, no stub SRAM. This is arithmetic from existing linked layout, NOT an actual relink; LTO partition changes, generated far-call trampolines, alignment, relocation and .data LMA can change footprint. All estimates below 0x3E000 boot boundary. For better padding select stable per-function input sections rather than rely permanently on ltrans index; don't assume simply --section-start will make space in overlapping .text. Research-only custom script preserving vectors, near PROGMEM, startup order, compiler trampoline range, .data initialization, noinit and EEPROM; ASSERT address/size, no overlap, end below boot boundary, audit exact ELF/HEX bytes and control flow for all four, ordinary CI and pinned compiler variants. Verify actual re-link and emulated bootloader WDRF path with/without stub before selecting implementation; no physical reset tests, no production patch this turn. 

### A17 owner-approved staged watchdog and application recovery implementation (2026-11-26)

Owner approved fail-safe over availability (a power-cycle-required reset loop is acceptable only with verified heater OFF), staged independent supervisor/internal reset, and the application trampoline. Explicitly document the nonobvious address in source so future cleanup cannot remove it. No device tests or default enable approved.

Verified predecessor: [CI 45816574847](https://github.com/Nibbels/Repetier-Firmware/actions/runs/45816574847) green on 9421fb7a, tested merge 46491ae2cf769624871929a052631a84226838bc. Artifact 15116017297 downloaded and independently SHA256 22223def4d546ea511fd2f5ed79c7e7e597273836524f3b5a4f3541d0406cdbf. Four regular, four lease-only, four reset-WDT builds and actual-source 4,587,520 HAL grant/expiry combinations passed, including witnessed-wrap checks. This is A16 evidence, not A17. Regular Flash/SRAM 213010/217350/217354/212376 and 5935/5985/6103/6019 B. A16 lease-only Flash 214830/219262/219266/214230, SRAM 5947/6003/6121/6034. Reset-only Flash 214936/219382/219386/214336, SRAM 5950/6006/6124/6037. Actual A16 linked WDR locations include early .init3 disable sequence, avr-libc enable and completed thermal feed; re-audit new images.

A17 candidate commits a1627cce, 94d4241a, 96d529be, a1647ffc: reset experiment borrows Timer5 /256 normal mode, compare A sticky flag, all compare pins disconnected, all Timer5 interrupts disabled (OC5A is RF1000 Z STEP). Absolute 12500-tick progress permission, 4-tick guard, no clearing expiry on renewal, 200ms nominal. Timer0 COMPB emits existing compensation event and WDI toggle on alternating 62/63 serviced ticks; TPS3820 needs falling edges, nominal falling edge spacing 32ms. Unlike old millis-based heartbeat, Timer5 independently refuses stale progress even after masked interrupts/multiple wraps, no further WDI edges or internal WDR after expiry; new heater grants and PWM rising edges blocked. TPS3820 timeout 112/200/300ms (current TI table at specified conditions, older rev E 310ms max at 25 C); intended external reset ~200ms progress + 112..300ms supervisor, internal WDTO_1S nominal backup. Ordering not guaranteed until actual clocks and board verified; no hard bound from nominal WDT oscillator. Runtime stop leaves WDI driven, revokes heaters, never disables supervisor by floating pin. Boot pre-start may float WDI with no heating allowed. Emergency software reset while watchdog active revokes outputs, masks PWM, waits with IRQs disabled for real reset, no feeds. Timer5 allocation is experimental and conflicts with three-channel Timer5 event heater PWM prototype; coordinate before final timer design. Existing periodic software PWM not yet replaced by independent hardware one-shot.

Recovery entry naked section .rf_watchdog_recovery: CLI, four immediate LDI/STS pairs writing 0xd39a6c52 to .noinit cookie, absolute JMP 0. Exactly 30 bytes, no stack use, no premature board-specific GPIO changes, no RET. Code directly documents STK500v2 EIND=1, byte vs word address, blob/issue and preservation contract. Mandatory undefined link guard referenced in .init3 so reset experiment cannot link by ordinary recipe without recovery script. Valid live-heating cookie 0xd39a6c51 written before positive grant and retained across zero-PID-duty phases with target/autotune active; best-effort .noinit evidence in addition to MCUSR. Recognized WDRF, recovery cookie or interrupted heat without known PORF latches existing heater/extrusion inhibit and reports error before command service. Cookie consumed once; unknown power cycle with retained SRAM and erased MCUSR may latch until another restart. Correct bootloaders may clear MCUSR and clobber SRAM, so cookie is NOT sole fail-off guarantee; no restoration of old targets/queue on any startup. Existing thermal latch inhibits heating/extrusion, not a new blanket motion interlock. No automatic stale reheating, but fresh host commands after unrecognized reset remain a concern requiring qualification.

acfa7449, 092cad55, 61a72391: optional linker script factory in baseline harness, ordinary recipe unchanged. Generate from selected avr-ld -m avr6 --verbose default script; select first LTO .text partition before location counter 0x20000, KEEP recovery, ASSERT address and 30-byte size, remaining .text after. Intentional padding first candidate, fail link if low region grows too far. Do not overwrite post-link code. Verify actual ELF .text and Intel HEX exact instruction bytes, checksums, unique address records, reset vector symbol, cookie SRAM range and image end below byte 0x3e000. Actual new linker validation still pending. Further check .noinit boundaries, linked EIND/SP startup, WDR count/write sequences, startup/control flow and no branch/fallthrough into recovery. Optimize padding only after correct relink. No production recipe altered.

d62559f6 extends actual HAL host extraction with Timer5 progress clock tests 524288 phases/elapsed cases per heater count, sticky expiry including 1/2 wraps, renewal guard, no rearm, cookie retention, initial PWM before start. Generic lease/ADC cases intentionally freeze Timer5 to isolate that component. a7d0f8b8, 9c0e37db, 616eaf80, 1cbb9aff: close autotune/periodic service phase mismatch. Shared progress byte bit7 is completed regular thermal pass; only consume with fresh per-channel grants on actual feed. Sampled autotune completion may finish the pending pass, but cannot manufacture a regular pass. Actual-source replay tests both orderings. No WDR from generic heartbeat. A17 CI [45819992703](https://github.com/Nibbels/Repetier-Firmware/actions/runs/45819992703) in progress for 1cbb9aff when recorded; previous 45819729688 cancelled by newer commits, no new pass claim. Inspect latest artifacts/errors before further changes or selection.

Remaining blockers: real board WDI routing RF2000 PC0 drawing vs PA7 code and v2; reset OFF bias RF1000/v2, fitted bootloader GPIO and RWW behavior, WDT oscillator/timing bounds, cold autotune admission at wrap, actual linked critical-path timing and CPU/foreground jitter, full max-duty hardware pulse shutoff where routed and PE2 independent power path, power-switch stuck ON independent thermal limit. Old bootloader reset loop cannot be rescued by external reset (WDRF sticky, WDI floats), acceptable only after physical OFF proof. Trampoline covers specific known EICALL wrong-target case, not every possible bootloader or arbitrary memory corruption. No merge or flashing.

Reset-WDT Flash/SRAM for RF1000/RF2000/RF2000v2/X44: 220910/224606/224606/222410 B and 5950/6006/6124/6037 B; padding 5402/4606/4602/7498 B, cookie SRAM addresses 0x1939/0x1971/0x19e7/0x1990 respectively; last application bytes 0x35eed/0x36d5d/0x36d5d/0x364c9, below boot boundary 0x3e000. 30-byte entry exact at byte 0x20000 in ELF and HEX; .noinit 5 bytes includes four-byte cookie and reset cause. A17 tested recovery-linked disassembly includes precisely three WDR opcodes per board: early disable at d41c/d9b4/d9bc/d77e; thermal feed f634/fd64/fd6c/fa7e; startup enable 11b84/127b4/127b4/11ffc. All timed writes actual opcodes verified: early WDR; LDI r24,0x18; STS 0x60,r24; STS 0x60,r1. Enable LDI r24,0x18; LDI r25,0x0e; IN r0,SREG; CLI; WDR; STS 0x60,r24; OUT SREG,r0; STS 0x60,r25 (outer lock holds IRQ off). Reset-only 1s nominal, not proven oscillator bound. Last instruction before each padding gap is an unconditional RJMP within GCodeInput::executeFlashScript, no fallthrough. No direct branch/call into padding or recovery entry in decoded instruction scan. Not proof of arbitrary indirect target safety. Actual opcode replay tools/task0034/boot-recovery-replay.cjs (24bf3862), executed locally in JS against fetched pinned ArduinoCore-avr 1.8.6 bootloader blob 68d2e18b and the 30 actual verified recovery bytes from A17 artifact, 36 cases per board, 144 total; 80 WDRF combinations across four images, 64 non-WDRF combinations. Nine MCUSR combinations (0,1,2,4,8,9,10,12,15), four deterministic initial SRAM/register byte patterns. Starts actual byte 0x3e000 reset vector, executes real AVR CRT, BSS initialization, bootloader prologue, WDT disable sequence, WDRF SBRS branch/EICALL and actual CLI/LDI/STS/JMP recovery. WDRF path reaches byte 0x20000 with EIND=1 and WDTCSR=0 under model, writes correct cookie, IRQ masked, reaches byte zero after 138 executed instructions (128 bootloader + 10 recovery). No-WDRF path stops at 0x3e724 without entering recovery. Does NOT model cycle timing, RWW/flash availability, hardware reset, watchdog oscillator/timed write window, actual interrupt delivery, external watchdog or electrical output; does not execute subsequent application startup or claim old 2011 bootloop rescue. Unsupported opcode fails. CI integration 380566c3 uses actual installed pinned Arduino core HEX, validates checksum and records SHA256 and report, verifies each actual linked recovery image; new CI pending. No full-system AVR emulator qualification yet.

Remaining software priorities: preserve full experimental ELF/HEX/maps in evidence archive (currently disassembly and exact byte reports only), verify latest full CI, then audit Timer3/5 renewal guard and combined thermal timing including startup/idle/autotune/serial overload; no optimization of padding until safe build and no ABI/near-flash regression. ### A19 green staged-watchdog checkpoint, renewal guard and Timer2 compare-clear research (2026-11-26)

[CI 45821345627](https://github.com/Nibbels/Repetier-Firmware/actions/runs/45821345627) fully green at head 17e27fa4, tested merge 154f319a913cd510f876d17ce881f69a94f9da54; [artifact 15118262275](https://github.com/Nibbels/Repetier-Firmware/actions/runs/45821345627/artifacts/15118262275), downloaded and independently verified SHA256 8b71ed6a8df21cb76bbc544ac7dcf21fa5588f3d96eaaf26ba3c3606acec2897. summary.json failures empty. All twelve AVR builds, all host tests including corrected legacy extraction, all four exact recovery ELF/HEX/linked WDR audits and 144 restricted boot opcode cases passed. Pinned bootloader file SHA256 081bd53f385df747b03bdafe7e154c1f72589f490be8138f3c4cc5517c93253b. Reset-WDT Flash/SRAM and recovery/padding identical to A18. This is a verified software checkpoint, not production or electrical qualification.

Actual linked critical renewal timing: extracted four reset-WDT disassemblies from A18 artifact, reproduced conservative 280 CPU cycles (17.5us) from active Timer3 TCNT3 low-byte snapshot to first OCR3 low-byte commit, and 80 cycles (5us) from Timer5 TCNT5 low-byte snapshot to OCR5A low-byte commit, identical on four variants. Charge EVERY instruction five cycles, include called leaseLive leaf 185 cycles (all leaf instructions plus two extra three-instruction shift loop iterations; validated channel <=2), trace branch/skip CFG to first write, reject other cyclic commit paths. Bound holds with IRQ lock and pinned instruction paths, vs 1024-cycle (64us) four-tick guard. Not a bound on entire setter, ADC witness scan, PWM rising edge, thermal service or interrupt latency. Reproducible script tools/task0034/renew-bound.py (a78b89e4) equivalent to locally executed audit; CI invocation c75171a8. Need still bound consume-to-arm and lease snapshot to latest physical HIGH, and measure complete regular/autotune foreground and IRQ jitter. 0303e1df/c75171a8 preserve eight experimental actual ELF, application HEX (not with-bootloader image), map, disassembly, stats, generated script and build properties under host/experimental-avr in future CI artifacts. Research only; never flash.

Timer2 independent falling-edge proposal, not integrated into HAL: tools/task0034/CompareClearHotends.h (1160488f), AvrTimer2CompareClear.h (367fc844). OC2A PB4 hotend0, OC2B PH6 hotend1; both share normal synchronous Timer2 /1024, 64us ticks, 16.384ms wrap. No Timer2 IRQ and no automatic rising edge while armed. Candidate splits prior 32.768ms heater frame into two 16.384ms pulses, width=duty&0xfe ticks, full request 254/256=99.21875% ideal vs prior sustained 100%, 16.256ms nominal maximum HIGH per pulse; arming time may shorten or skip pulse, never catch up. Explicit OFF bias while DDR=input is required, NOT demonstrated for every fitted board (RF1000 no explicit gate pulldown in known drawing, RF2000 100k in drawing, v2 unknown). Arm: check previous active pulse's sticky compare flag; if unfinished, skip without renewal; PORT low; DDR input; COM SET; FOC; COM CLEAR; TCNT snapshot; OCR deadline; W1C only isolated channel; coherent remaining >1 and <=width and no pending flag; mark active, DDR output last. Any crash before final drive leaves pin OFF by bias; after drive, hardware CLEAR ends pulse if timer runs. Revoke writes PORT low before disconnecting OC, restores DDR output, clears active bit; interrupted revoke leaves at most old bounded pulse. Never switch a driven OC output to SET. Timer2 adapter rejects asynchronous/busy mode, disables IRQs, does not allocate Timer4/5. No actual silicon or electrical proof. Test tests/heater-compare-clear-prototype.py (24ca3c47) exercises actual template with abstract compare/latch/DDR/tick semantics: 256 phases *127 positive duty levels *3 operation-gap values *16 crash positions = 1,560,576 cases, plus independent two-channel and revoke tests; no pass claimed until CI. AVR linked probe f07b4708 asserts Timer2 vectors default and emits disassembly, no pulse timing claim yet. Consider Timer2 OVF as event-driven rearm scheduler (61.035/s, no compare ISR) versus existing Timer0 half-frame trigger; model late/skipped frames and power error, no catch-up. Need measure actual arming instruction bounds, compare/FOC semantics, pin maps and pin bias before integrating. All HAL hotend fail/revoke and software PWM paths must use OC-aware owner together (GPIO LOW alone cannot clear an OC override). RF1000 bed OC4C shares Timer4 RGB, not allocated here; RF2000/v2 bed PE2 no OC. Remain on independent lease+reset path until qualified alternative; no claim of interrupt-independent per-pulse falling edge for these bed paths.

Additional watchdog lifecycle defect: runtime stop set watchdogActive=false; a repeated stop would treat it as pre-start and float WDI, and subsequent resetHardware would fall back to software jump. ee9a762e introduces sticky watchdogStarted (one global byte in experiment only), prevents repeated start from rearming expired progress clock and repeated stop from floating WDI, preserves real-reset wait after any start. ba866a6f actual-source host test extracts complete stopWatchdog and Timer5 block, models WDI direction, tests two runtime stops and pre-start stop. New CI not yet complete; don't claim these changes verified. Existing production path unchanged. Next verify latest CI, new probe and actual resource/linked timing deltas. No merge or physical experiment.

Actual linked standalone Timer2 probe snapshot-to-drive bound: 150 cycles conservative (=9.375us at 16MHz), calculated locally against exact A19 disassembly by charging each instruction five cycles, traversing acyclic successful CFG from first TCNT2 read to call of drive(), charging every instruction in acyclic DDR leaf. One Timer2 /1024 tick =1024 cycles=64us; snapshot-to-drive shorter than one tick; accepted modular remaining >1. Reproduce with tools/task0034/compare-arm-bound.py (43efa02b), pinned probe CI invocation ff42a60c. Bound is for isolated linked adapter/probe, NOT integrated frame/lease checks or full-system WCET. Real linked final candidate must re-establish it. Datasheet 2549P section 20.5/20.5.1/20.5.3: compare flag on next timer clock, FOC in non-PWM updates OC latch without setting compare flag, CPU TCNT write suppresses compare on next clock (only initialization writes TCNT2 in this adapter; never on active pulse). Treat one additional compare tick conservatively: max nominal high 255 ticks=16.320ms, not 16.256ms as absolute claim. Timer must run, external OFF bias while DDR input required, no arbitrary register corruption or stuck-on switch guarantee.

cbc36319 extends actual-template replay to immediate and one-tick delayed compare action (abstract continuous TCNT/OCR comparator with mode at action), all phases/duties/gaps/crash prefixes, and completed-pulse rearm with OC CLEAR still attached. Locally reconstructed same C++ executable from repo template and updated test statements, g++ -std=c++11 -O2 -Wall -Wextra -Werror passed 3,121,152 crash-prefix cases (1,142,784 produced heat; max255 ticks), 104,448 completed-pulse rearm cases, two-channel and revoke checks. This is local reconstructed-source evidence, not CI of exact committed files yet; run 45824019999 pending before docs commit. No production HAL change this round. Need verify final CI including renewal-bound script, then compare fixed Timer2 OVF rearm vs Timer0 serviced half-frame scheduling with jitter and skipped pulses, quantify actual delivered energy/CPU cost and verify all positive and revoke paths in combined opt-in HAL before considering adoption. Keep compare-clear gated on electrical OFF bias; no assumption of RF1000/v2 qualification. No merge, no device flash.

### A21 owner requirement — separate electrical switching and thermal response envelopes (2026-11-26)

Owner explicitly requests exploiting DC hotend versus AC heated-bed differences and preserving/reviewing the entire thermal supervision (rise, hold, maximum, dead time, emergency shutdown). Owner's approximate bed rise ~1 C/s (roughly half to twice that) is a hypothesis for simulation sensitivity, not a measured universal threshold. Small hotend block, fast response, fan/extrusion disturbances; large bed, slower and spatially nonuniform thermal response, sensor-to-surface delay. Separate actuator technology from thermal plant profile: a DC retrofit bed is still thermally a bed, and installed hotend cartridges/blocks/sensors vary. No new production thresholds or hardware tests approved.

Electrical evidence: [RF2000 Conrad schematic I851 CTC V3.0 sheet 6](https://asset.conrad.com/media10/add/160267/c1/-/en/001395717CD01/schema-electrique-1395717-renkforce-rf2000-imprimante-3d-appareil-pret-a-lemploi-banc-dimpression-chauffant.pdf) reviewed visually again: HZ1/HZ2 MOSFETs Q3/Q4 (CSD18531Q5A), low-side VM1 DC, HZ3 mains mat X38 via MOC3061SVM zero-cross optotriac and BTA12-600BWRG triac, parallel optional DC output X10 Q5, HZ1/2/3 100k pulldown R121/122/123. 24V DC common reported for RF2000v2 and RFx000 hotend retrofits, not a confirmed voltage for every installed cartridge or board. [RF2000 manual technical data p141](https://www.manualshelf.com/manual/renkforce/renkforce-rf2000/3-user-manual-german/page-141.html) specifies 450W bed; [RF2000v2 manual p99](https://www.manualslib.de/manual/705801/Renkforce-Rf2000-V2.html?page=99) 560W bed, mains 230V/50Hz. At 50Hz resistive AC power is delivered in ~10ms half-cycles, a normal conducting triac remains conducting until current zero after GPIO off (nominal <=10ms residual, not protection against shorted switch). Do not claim exact line synchronization from a free-running AVR timer: no verified zero-cross input. Evaluate longer bounded time-proportioning windows or distributed complete cycles where synchronized, sweep unknown AC phase and 50/60Hz, avoid systematic polarity bias; never extend a high pulse beyond valid lease or compensate missed pulses with excess energy. DC hotend compare-clear remains conditional on OFF bias and running timer. Do not sacrifice fresh ADC/thermal progress deadlines merely because bed is slow. Earlier A19/A20 compare-clear prototype is research-only.

Actual source audit (TemperatureControl.cpp, Extruder.h, Extruder.cpp, Configuration.h on current task branch): regular manageTemperatures nominal 100ms; per-controller decoupleTestPeriod initialized from EXT0/EXT1/BED_DECOUPLE_TEST_PERIOD all default 12000ms (may be configurable via existing settings); common DECOUPLING_TEST_MIN_TEMP_RISE=1C and DECOUPLING_TEST_MAX_HOLD_VARIANCE=20C. Rise phase entered when target error >20C and output >0, not proof of actual full power; after each successful rise reset baseline. Hold entry only within 19C, first check after configured period, then successful checks roughly 1s. One shared extruderTempErrors counter >10 latches confirmed fault; errorDetected suppresses later decoupling checks in same pass, clean pass decrements counter. Invalid sensor sample immediately revokes affected power, then debounces diagnostics/fault; type0 disabled never heats. PID derivative filtered 0.5s and integration uses actual elapsed <=1000ms; filteredSlope is PID state, NOT a maximum-rise safety check. Above heaterCutoffC regular PID only forces duty zero; it is not itself an immediate latched overtemperature event if sensor value remains otherwise plausible. setTargetTemperature stops decouple observation, target below previous/zero immediately revokes, target below MAX_ROOM_TEMPERATURE stops decouple in manage; no dedicated post-revoke/off-rise test. Consequently stuck switch or external heating may remain undetected while target zero except broad sensor limits; a detached sensor may remain electrically plausible; small target changes may reset baseline; shared fault count/order can mask or mix channel evidence. Need explicit tests for all, not simply tighten generic thresholds. Existing confirmed thermal fault Printer::latchOutputInhibit(true) revokes all heater outputs under IRQ exclusion, sets persistent-until-reset thermal inhibit, inhibits extrusion; stopPrint discards print queue, preserve cooling and diagnostics. This is not a hardware mains emergency stop nor guaranteed protection from stuck power semiconductor. Autotune already distinguishes bed/hotend: rise check 60s/20s for common 1C, target-relative cutoff +15/+20C bounded by absolute cutoff, total/phase limits 60min/10min vs 20min/3min; cutoff/timeout paths abort tune, no-rise latches global fault. Audit consistency including first invalid sample, measured above absolute cutoff and expected overshoot, no-rise, excessive rise, hold drift, sensor setting changes, fresh leases, idle/off, pause/reheat, autotune, startup, repeated target edits, actual delivered duty and missing services.

Next work before default enable: separate per-heater thermal supervision owner from PID calculation and from actuator; model explicit OFF/HEATING/APPROACH/HOLD/COOLDOWN and latched FAULT transitions or equivalent small state machine. Independently preserve hard absolute max, fresh/raw plausibility, immediate revoke; select per-heater minimum/maximum plausible rise, power-conditioned rise/timeout, sensor/plant delay and hold bands using simulation envelope then device qualification (do not silently narrow based on one guessed slope). Track admitted pulse energy separately from requested PID duty, account for skipped frames/AC uncertainty; no measured-current assumption. Off-state rise test must allow stored heat after shutoff and cross-heating, while flagging persistent incompatible trend. A single sensor cannot prove actual heater temperature or detect every detached/failed-closed switch; independent physical thermal cutoff necessary. Keep current EEPROM compatibility and report SRAM/cycle costs; prioritize simple bounded foreground state, avoid float work in ISRs. Create deterministic multi-node thermal plant scenarios (heater, sensor, bed surface, ambient/airflow/filament draw), vary thermal capacitance, coupling, delay, supply, gain, initial T, disturbances, sensor faults, stuck-off/on, stale samples, AC phase and scheduler jitter. Report false alarms and time/energy/peak temperature to detection for fault scenarios separately; model results cannot establish real safety limits. Do not use normal PID/autotune as fault supervisor, do not weaken prior thermal protection, do not merge until checks. A21 is requirements and source audit only, no firmware changes.

### A22 owner requirement and initial PID/thermal parameter sweep (2026-11-26)

Owner explicitly requires alternative hotends (e.g. 15 W vs own 40 W cartridge) not to trip normal safety checks merely for different power, mass or heating slope. Reports historical retrofit false-positive bug, exact report not yet located. Current community_development Configuration.h has same 1C/12s/+-20C defaults; GitHub searches for decouple/decoupling issues/commits returned no results. Related but NOT confirmed same bug: RF1000 forum [Mein Umbau auf Ganzmetallhotend, 2017](https://www.rf1000.de/viewtopic.php?t=2054) includes E3D PID integral-limit/temperature convergence complaints. No claim about historical threshold change. Use PC-side PID + plant simulations to derive cheap robust rules (slope/dead-time/power windows, approach/hold/off); avoid online complex model in AVR, do not infer wattage or a physically safe thermal envelope from PID gains alone. Configurable profiles or explicitly validated calibration observations may be useful; learning must never excuse existing faults or relax hard absolute temperature and hardware lease/reset limits. Preserve a safe fallback for unknown hardware. No production limit selected.

Implemented and executed deterministic Node-compatible tools/task0034/thermal-plant-sweep.cjs at 1cab909f, exact committed source re-executed via JS. Model two energy-conserving lumped masses (heater and body), lagged sensor, ambient 25 C, averaged masked PWM, 100ms PID update mirroring TemperatureControl.cpp derivative-on-measurement 0.5s, integral clamp/antiwindup and configured output cap; JS double precision not extracted AVR source or float32 replay. Uses repo hotend v1/v2/v3 and bed 53.74/7.48/96.52 or 34/0.015/110 gain sets from Configuration.h/RF1000.h/RF2000.h. Gains are crossed with plant variations, NOT assumed suitable calibration for every heater. Hypothetical sweep hotend 15/25/40/60W, block/body 6/12/24 J/K, heater 1.5 J/K, coupling 2/6 W/K, loss .035/.07 W/K, sensor tau .25/1.5s, PID cap 128/255; bed 300/450/560W, body 300/600/1000 J/K, heater 40 J/K, coupling 8/20 W/K, loss 2/4 W/K, sensor tau 1/6s, cap 128/255. Target 200/90 C, 480/660s heat then 120s off, 2/30W load disturbance before final 30s hold, 0.1C quantized measurement. Euler 0.05s, representative convergence at 0.025s (max difference first 12s rise 0.2C; peak body 0.029C). Hypothetical parameters and coarse model, not measured properties; no real thermal qualification, spatial bed gradients, radiation nonlinearities, actual pulse timing/AC phase, ADC sample/lease timing, or full firmware/concurrent-channel replay. Fixed gains across plants, not simulation of actual autotune yet. Model cutoff 275/180 C and observational single-channel legacy rise/hold counter, no fault feedback so compare on identical traces. Temperature after a modeled fault is not a prediction of actual device peak; model does not model material failure or independent cutoff. Settled descriptor: reaches target within 1C at some time and max error <=5C in final 30s after load disturbance removed; NOT acceptance criteria.

864 healthy-plant parameter combinations: 576 hotend, 288 bed. 431/92 settled (145/196 not settled; distinguish underpowered, output limit and incompatible gains from failed heater; no claim all combinations are realistic). Old detector flags 60/50 cases, among settled 0/1. Settled 12s rise ranges hotend 5.7..90.3C, bed .8..14.7C; max observed 1-second rise .6..8 / .2..1.6C; time to first within 1C 24.5..460.6 / 48.6..471.7s. Counterexample healthy settled 15W 6 J/K fast case rises 22.6C first 12s vs 40W 24 J/K slower case 7.6C (different limit/coupling etc; not isolated mass experiment). 450W 1000 J/K capped slow bed reaches setpoint band after 471.7s but old detector trips at 13.1s, final hold error .2C on observational trace. Do not select tighter global slope cutoff from these data. Model normal post-off rebound 0..0.1/0..0.4C; no general safety threshold from simplistic model. Representative per power slow/fast settled traces 14, four injected faults each (56): stuck-off at 30s legacy trips 14/14; frozen plausible sensor at 30s 13/14; detached 14/14; stuck-on at scheduled off legacy 1/14 (the same preexisting settled-bed false alarm, no new off-state detection), broad model cutoff crossed 8/14 during 120s post-off; 10s post-off rise .5..67.4C under stuck-on. No actual current sensing, off-monitor cannot itself interrupt failed-closed power switch. Need expanded fault timing across near target, hold, pause, slow drift, sensor placement, target edits, fan/transient loads and cross-heating, independently tuned PID/autotune, uncertainty and numerical convergence. Then compare simple per-heater state-machine rules for false-positive and missed/late detections, separate hard safety guards from identification. No new firmware cutoff yet.

A21 [CI 45825333632](https://github.com/Nibbels/Repetier-Firmware/actions/runs/45825333632) failed only heater-lease-integrated-avr; artifact 15120795123 SHA256 per GitHub b70d4164345bd1cb3eb99b3ed124f018ff433b79841fdd2cf0e519ba956d3841. Timer2 extended actual-source replay passed 3,121,152 crash prefixes and 104,448 rearm, linked probe passed. Reset RF1000 and RF2000 built and archived; audit stopped on RF2000's two 0x95a8 *data words* decoded as WDR in GCodeInput::parseAscii. Actual bounded switch dispatch at byte 0x12a88..0x12aa2 indexes 81 16-bit word-address entries at 0x12aa2..0x12b44, uses __tablejump2__ (actual 8 instructions ELPM/EIJMP); both 0x95a8 entries point to byte 0x12b50, valid case code, NOT watchdog instructions. RF1000 table 0x11e72..0x11f14. 815f4097 adds narrowly recognized opcode/operand/length/base/count checks and verifies all entries target instruction boundaries in same function outside table; rejects direct branch into table or recovery/padding; does not whitelist parser function or arbitrary extra WDR. Locally executed identical recognition on both archived actual disassemblies, three genuine WDR sites each. Not general disassembly of all indirect paths or corrupted PCs. ### A23 owner decision — autotune characterization and latched thermal emergency diagnostics (2026-11-26)

Owner endorses automatic thermal characterization during the four existing autotune profiles (J0/J1 hotend, J2/J3 bed), especially to support retrofits from weak 15 W cartridges to high-power low-mass modern E3D designs. Avoid mandatory manual wattage/mass configuration. Expand offline plant sweeps to high power/low thermal inertia, rapid sensor response, overshoot, power cap and disturbances; don't assume all new designs are compatible with board current, connector or cartridge voltage ratings. Characterize measured response during valid controlled heating/cooling phases and use conservative profile-dependent margins; known PID equations/gains alone do not identify sensor/plant safety. Calibration must pass independent plausibility, absolute limits, fresh authorization, sufficient excitation and repeatability checks; never learn from a fault, never automatically widen limits after a trip, never change the independent fail-off deadlines. Persist only validated complete calibration linked to heater/sensor/power limit and validity/version (EEPROM compatibility/atomicity and resource cost to be audited). Keep conservative unknown-profile behavior; no unqualified numerical envelope or production changes selected.

Owner requests restoring the beeper exclusively as a loud thermal-emergency annunciator, no ordinary key/menu/print sounds, no revival of old feature flags or user-disable preference. Exact alarm pattern deferred. Historical community_development Repetier/pins.h defines BEEPER_PIN_RF1000=23 and BEEPER_PIN_RF2000=5, RF1000 BEEPER_PIN=23 (Arduino Mega PA1), RF2000 and RF2000v2 BEEPER_PIN=5 (PE3). Current per-board pin definitions have no obvious competing assignment to these same pins (other board's LCD/key can use the number; not a conflict within a board). Historical ui.cpp beep(duration,count) defaults non-inverting active HIGH, toggles with blocking delays and checks enableBeeper; do NOT restore this implementation. Verify fitted sounder/driver/polarity and pin mapping before use. Initialize inactive early, alarm service nonblocking and bounded, no busy waits and no additional high-frequency IRQ unless necessary/qualified. Active/passive sounder behavior unqualified. Alarm is diagnostic, not a safety interlock, no claim of sound if CPU/power/display fails. Keep task #91 UI work held; coordinate any actual shared UI patch.

Explicit distinction: software-detected thermal emergency is a latched diagnostic operating mode, NOT an intentional watchdog reset or ordinary stop/idle. First synchronously revoke all heater authorization and actual outputs (OC-aware), prevent rearm and all pending/new heat, print, motion, homing, extrusion, pause/reheat, scan, macro, manual/UI, autotune and settings side effects; discard queued and direct moves through appropriate safe stop path, no new movement. Assess safe stepper hold/deenergization and Z/load hazard rather than blindly drop holding torque. Keep ADC/sensor supervision, necessary hotend/case cooling (conservative cooling if sensor invalid), LCD, buzzer and bounded read-only serial diagnostics alive. For the still-functioning CPU continue watchdog servicing ONLY after actual fault-mode thermal/diagnostic safety pass confirms all software heat requests and actuator state OFF; never bypass WDT with generic heartbeat or unconditional feed. If diagnostics CPU hangs, independent watchdog reset still required, recovery must not auto-resume heating. A dead CPU cannot guarantee LCD/beeper; best effort reset cause/cookie and future retained fault evidence, not guaranteed for all bootloaders. A potentially shorted power switch cannot be made safe by software duty=0; instruct immediate safe power isolation for suspected uncontrolled heating/smoke; independent cutoff remains required.

Record cause and affected channel(s) BEFORE clearing setpoints/duties: distinguish sensor invalid/stale, insufficient/excessive rise, hold drift, absolute overtemperature, rising while commanded OFF, expired lease/watchdog recovery, calibration mismatch etc. Preserve first-fault context (measured temperature if valid, requested target/duty, elapsed/observed delta) plus later fault masks per channel; do not overwrite with secondary shutdown consequences. LCD emergency screen must remain dominant over normal menu/Ready/stop status, present E0/E1/Bed and specific reason(s), plus current/last valid measured temperature clearly marked, compact 16x2 and 20x4 layouts or bounded cycling, no scrolling truncation of critical channel ID. User suggests Firewarning and hint check autotune after retrofit: use unambiguous thermal emergency/fire risk wording, not claim actual fire sensed; only offer retune after power-off inspection, correction and deliberate recovery, never a button to bypass live latch or retune while fault latched. Preserve diagnostics on USB too; ensure serial backpressure cannot prevent safe-mode progress. Reset/ack policy deliberate, no automatic retry, no reopening heat via M999 or menu; explicit cold inspected restart/requalification. No EEPROM writes in shutdown critical path; persistent logs optional separate design.

Source audit: current TemperatureControl.cpp Printer::latchOutputInhibit(true) sets shared temperature defect and extrusion SafetyLock, revokes outputs under IRQ protection then zeros targets. stopPrint() in Printer.cpp is normal job-abort path with beginStop() early return, pause handling, queued-move discard and status, not global durable emergency command lock. Commands::commandLoop currently admits commands unless printJob.commandsBlocked(); checkForPeriodicalActions still calls normal UI, compensation, DMS, feature and scan services. Need explicit emergency admission at all relevant owners, not rely on ordinary stop. UiFormatting.cpp renderTemperatureField %ew already lists flagged E0/E1/Bed, normal numeric temperature fields are suppressed for global defect. Lease/recovery latch can lack per-channel flags; current display is inadequate for specific fault cause and live diagnostic temperature. Retain/extend existing reporting rather than simply reuse generic def/dec. Existing alarm function removed; introduce small safety-specific owner with nonblocking service and documented physical assumptions. Verify no runtime transition can clear latch, first-fault retention/multiple reasons, emergency at idle/during print/pause/scan/autotune, command/USB/UI injection, fault-mode actual WDT progress and stall, all four board builds and LCD widths, no unintended beeps in normal state. This entry is design/requirements and actual-source audit only, not implementation or physical qualification.

CI checkpoint: [45828745034](https://github.com/Nibbels/Repetier-Firmware/actions/runs/45828745034) on b648fc10 completed successfully, confirming A22 switch-table audit fix in full suite; preserve evidence and examine resource/timing deltas before further firmware changes. No merge or flash.

### A24 owner clarification — severity-specific alarm and mandatory USB fault log

Supersedes generic FIRE WARNING examples in A23: reserve the exact prominent FIRE WARNING text for measured overtemperature or credible excessive/uncommanded heating (e.g. confirmed excessive rise or continued rise incompatible with residual heat and OFF command). Do not label ordinary no-rise, sensor invalid/stale, hold failure, calibration mismatch, lease expiry or watchdog recovery as fire warnings solely because they cause a thermal emergency. All confirmed thermal emergencies still require the same latched heater/operation inhibit and safety-only audible alarm; wording reflects observed evidence, not proof of actual fire. Severity may escalate if additional credible evidence arrives after the original fault; preserve original cause.

Owner requires serial/USB diagnostic output because printers are connected to Repetier. Mandatory first-fault report with affected channel(s), stable cause/severity, uptime, original target, measured temperature (validity/raw ADC as applicable), requested and admitted duty if available, observed delta/window, violated threshold and active calibration/profile identity as applicable; distinguish unknown/invalid values and commanded OFF from measured physical power (no current feedback). Capture minimal immutable evidence before zeroing targets/duties, with no delay to physical revoke; format/output only AFTER fail-off and latch. Retain per-channel additional fault evidence and severity escalation, rate-limited live temperatures and repeat/read-only diagnostic access for reconnect; avoid log floods. Use existing Repetier-compatible error reporting and host cancellation conventions (RequestStop:, // action:cancel); audit parser/ACK compatibility, keep read-only temperature/diagnostics available and verify stalled/full TX or disconnected host cannot delay shutdown, cooling, thermal sampling or watchdog progress. No assumption host receives every byte or persists logs; LCD remains independent local indicator. Retune hint only for relevant faults and only after inspection/cold deliberate recovery, never a live override. Examples: THERMAL ERROR / E0: NO RISE; FIRE WARNING / BED: OVER TEMP. This is a requirements clarification, not implementation; no new thresholds, merge or hardware test.

### A35 in-flight EEPROM integrity and autotune unwind (2026-11-26)

Source audit: EEPROM::clearEEPROM is called from EEPROM::init only, during Printer::setup before HAL::setupTimer/startWatchdog; do not make a partial wipe look successful. Runtime EEPROM::updateChecksum computes modulo-256 sum over 2048 bytes except integrity cell 104, yields to periodic service, and writes the checksum AFTER settings bytes. A terminal thermal fault at a checksum yield must not truncate that sum. Finish the integrity scan/byte, continue emergency thermal service at existing yields, then unwind callers. This is not power-loss atomicity (old additive checksum has collisions and multi-byte writes are not journaled). No EEPROM layout change. 25294037 adds terminal checks at loader entry and after both potential checksum/store yields, before ordinary derived parameter/Extruder/Advance/bed initialization. f7275c7e suppresses normal M500/M501/M502 success/log/UI after fault; 253c97d7 similarly for panel restore defaults. f8bd1eae stops active autotune immediately after a nested periodic fault, after calibration-store checksum fault, and before scheduling normal Ready; no legacy showError after terminal latch. finishAutotune still revokes output and clears autotune owner, case cooling follows existing emergency guard. No new fault limits or sensor algorithm.
